<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the modularity conjecture for elliptic curves over imaginary quadratic fields: every such curve is modular, with matching local parameters at every place.

- **claim-main** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $K$ be an imaginary quadratic number field and $E/K$ an elliptic curve. There is an isobaric automorphic representation $\pi_E$ of $\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_2(\mathbb A_K)$ with trivial central character and weight zero such that, for every prime $l$ and every isomorphism $\iota:\overline\mathbb Q_l\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb C$, $$r_\iota(\pi_E)\simeq H^1_{\mathrm{\acute et}}(E_{\overline K},\overline\mathbb Q_l).$$ At every finite place $v\nmid l$ one has $$\iota\mathop{\mathrm{WD}}\bigl(H^1_{\mathrm{\acute et}}(E_{\overline K},\overline\mathbb Q_l) \|_{G_{K_v}}\bigr)^{\mathrm{F\text{-}ss}} \simeq \mathop{\mathrm{rec}}_{K_v}\bigl(\pi_{E,v}\otimes\|\det\|_v^{-1/2}\bigr).$$ The unitary parameter at the complex place is $z\mapsto\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(z/\|z\|,\overline z/\|z\|)$. In particular every local factor agrees, and $$L(E/K,s)=L(\pi_E,s-1/2).$$ If $E$ has no complex multiplication, $\pi_E$ is cuspidal. In the CM case it is given by automorphic induction of a Hecke character, or by an isobaric sum when the CM field is already $K$.
  - Explanation: Every elliptic curve over an imaginary quadratic field matches an isobaric automorphic representation of weight zero. The match covers the l-adic cohomology and the local parameters at all places. The representation is cuspidal without complex multiplication and is an induction or isobaric sum in the CM case.
- **claim-kummer-family** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-family)): For each divisor $m>1$ of $n$, there is a smooth proper family of geometrically connected genus-$m$ curves $\mathcal C_m\to U$. It is the normalization of the smooth proper elliptic family $B\to U$ in the function-field extension, written fiberwise as $$\label{geo:kummer} Y^m=f_t(Z),\qquad \operatorname{div}(f_t)=(S_t)-(-S_t)-n(R_t)+n(-R_t),\qquad f_t(O)=1.$$ The family has an involution $$\label{geo:reflection} w:(Z,Y)\longmapsto(-Z,Y^{-1}).$$ Over a field containing $\mu_m$, rotations $u_\zeta:(Z,Y)\mapsto(Z,\zeta Y)$ are defined and satisfy $wu_\zeta w=u_{\zeta^{-1}}$. If $d\mid m$, the map $Y\mapsto Y^{m/d}$ gives the quotient $C_{m,t}\to C_{d,t}$; for $d=1$ the quotient is $B_t$.
  - Explanation: Normalising the moving elliptic family in a Kummer extension gives a smooth proper family of connected curves whose genus equals the divisor. The family carries an inverting involution and rotations.
- **claim-semistable-models** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-semistable-models)): In the constant-$E$ family, blow up the surface at the collision point over either boundary parameter in <a href="#geo:boundary-points" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="geo:boundary-points">[geo:boundary-points]</a>, and normalize the resulting surface in $Y^m=f_R$. This gives a regular semistable model near that parameter. Its special fiber has $m$ geometric nodes lying over the node of the blown-up elliptic surface. Each node has smoothing parameter of order one in a parameter on the base. The component above the exceptional projective line is a geometrically connected rational curve.
  - Explanation: Blowing up the colliding sections and normalising in the cyclic cover gives a regular semistable model. Its special fibre has nodes with smoothing parameter of order one.
- **claim-compact-type** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-compact-type)): Suppose that $k$ contains $\mu_m$. At $R=O$, the special fiber of the model in Lemma <a href="#geo:semistable-models" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geo:semistable-models">3</a> is a rational curve joined at distinct points to $m$ copies of $E$, all defined over $k$. Rotations cyclically permute the elliptic components. The Jacobian family of $C_{m,R}$ therefore extends to an abelian scheme on a Zariski neighborhood of $O$ in the parameter curve, with fiber $E^m$ at $O$.
  - Explanation: At the origin the special fibre is a rational curve joined to copies of the elliptic curve with a tree dual graph. The Jacobian family therefore extends to an abelian scheme with product fibre.
- **claim-collision** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-collision)): For $R_*=a/(2n)$, the special fiber in Lemma <a href="#geo:semistable-models" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geo:semistable-models">3</a> consists of a connected unramified degree-$m$ cover $E'\to E$ and a rational component, joined at $m$ distinct nodes. In analytic coordinates near each node, the family has equation $XU=-2nt$, where $t=R-R_*$ is an additive local parameter.
  - Explanation: At the second boundary point the special fibre joins a connected unramified cover of E to a rational curve at nodes with local equation of smoothing order one.
- **claim-slit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mon-slit)): For the parameters in <a href="#mon:horizontal-path" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mon:horizontal-path">[mon:horizontal-path]</a>, the cover $C_{m,R_s}\to E$ is trivial over $E\setminus I_s$. It is obtained by cutting $m$ copies of $E$ along $I_s$ and joining adjacent banks cyclically. Crossing the slit changes the sheet index by $1$ or $-1$, according to the orientation of crossing and the initial choice of indexing.
  - Explanation: Cutting copies of the torus along a horizontal arc and gluing adjacent banks cyclically realises the cover, which is trivial off the arc.
- **claim-integral-module** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mon-integral-module)): The cycles $a_i,b_i$ form an integral symplectic basis of $H_1(C_{m,R_s},\mathbb Z)$. Consequently, for every smooth fiber of the family, $$\label{mon:free-lattices} H_1(C_m,\mathbb Z)\simeq\mathbb Z[\Gamma_m]^2, \qquad H^1(C_m,\mathbb Z)\simeq\mathbb Z[\Gamma_m]^2.$$ In particular, after comparison, $H^1_{\mathrm{et}}(C_m,\mathbb Z_\ell)$ is free of rank two over $\mathbb Z_\ell[\Gamma_m]$ for every prime $\ell$, including $\ell\mid m$.
  - Explanation: Slit-avoiding loops form an integral symplectic basis. Homology and cohomology are therefore free of rank two over the group ring, also at primes dividing the covering degree.
- **claim-vanishing-classes** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mon-vanishing-classes)): After a cyclic indexing of the lifts, their homology classes are $$\label{mon:delta} [\delta_i]=\pm(a_i-a_{i+1}),\qquad i\in\mathbb Z/m\mathbb Z,$$ or the same formula with $i-1$ in place of $i+1$.
  - Explanation: The vanishing cycles at the nodes are differences of adjacent horizontal generators in the integral basis, up to sign.
- **claim-transvections** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mon-transvections)): In one integral basis $a_i,b_i$ as above, the geometric monodromy of the constant-$E$ family contains operators $T_a,T_b$ satisfying $$\begin{aligned} T_a(a_j)&=a_j,& T_a(b_j)&=b_j+\sigma_a(2a_j-a_{j-1}-a_{j+1}), \label{mon:Ta}\\ T_b(b_j)&=b_j,& T_b(a_j)&=a_j+\sigma_b(2b_j-b_{j-1}-b_{j+1}), \label{mon:Tb} \end{aligned}$$ where $\sigma_a,\sigma_b\in\{1,-1\}$ are independent of $j$. Equivalently, their two off-diagonal entries, in the basis of the free $\mathbb Z[\Gamma_m]$-module, are $\sigma_a(2-u-u^{-1})$ and $\sigma_b(2-u-u^{-1})$. The geometric monodromy of the family over $U$ contains these actions as well, under the pullback identification.
  - Explanation: Picard-Lefschetz twists at the nodes give two unipotent operators in one common integral basis. Their off-diagonal entries are the cyclic difference operator up to a sign.
- **claim-system-properties** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sys-properties)): For every $t\in U(K)$ and every rotation character $\theta$, the representations $V_{\theta,\ell}$ have the following properties.* 1. *They are semisimple of dimension two, with determinant $\epsilon_\ell^{-1}$.* 2. *At every place of $F$ above $\ell$, they are potentially semistable with labelled Hodge–Tate weights $\{0,1\}$.* 3. *There is a finite set of places $S$ of $F$, independent of $\ell$, such that $V_{\theta,\ell}$ is unramified at every $v\notin S$ of residue characteristic different from $\ell$. At such a place its Frobenius characteristic polynomial is the image under $j_\ell$ of a polynomial in $M[T]$ independent of $\ell$.
  - Explanation: Each rotation-character piece is a semisimple two-dimensional Galois representation with inverse cyclotomic determinant and Hodge-Tate weights zero and one. It is unramified outside a finite set with Frobenius polynomials independent of the coefficient prime.
- **claim-elliptic-realizations** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sys-elliptic)): If $\theta$ has order $m>1$ dividing $n$, the system $V_\theta$ is realized by the corresponding character space in $H^1(C_m)$. For the trivial character, $$V_{1,\ell}\simeq H^1_{\mathrm{\acute et}} (B_{t,\overline F},\overline\mathbb Q_\ell).$$ Let $D_t=C_3(t)/\langle w\rangle$ and $A_t=\mathop{\mathrm{Jac}}(D_t)$. Then $D_t$ has genus one, $A_t$ is an elliptic curve over $F$, and for either nontrivial order-three character $\eta$ there are $G_F$-equivariant isomorphisms $$V_{\eta,\ell}\simeq H^1_{\mathrm{\acute et}}(A_{t,\overline F},\overline\mathbb Q_\ell) \qquad\text{for every }\ell.$$
  - Explanation: The trivial-character piece is the cohomology of the twist. The order-three piece is the cohomology of the Jacobian of a genus-one quotient, an elliptic curve over F.
- **claim-integral-evaluation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sys-integral-evaluation)): Fix $t\in U(K)$ and a prime $\ell$. Let $L/\mathbb Q_\ell$ be a finite extension containing $j_\ell(M)$, with ring of integers $\mathcal O$, maximal ideal $\mathfrak m$, and residue field $k$. For a rotation character $\theta$, the module $$\mathcal T_\theta= H^1_{\mathrm{\acute et}}(C_{n,\overline F},\mathbb Z_\ell) \otimes_{\mathbb Z_\ell[\mu_n],\,j_\ell\theta}\mathcal O$$ is a free rank-two $G_F$-stable lattice realizing $V_{\theta,\ell}$ after extension to $\overline\mathbb Q_\ell$. If $j_\ell\theta$ and $j_\ell\theta'$ have the same reduction as characters $\mu_n\to k^\times$, then $$\mathcal T_\theta/\mathfrak m\mathcal T_\theta \simeq \mathcal T_{\theta'}/\mathfrak m\mathcal T_{\theta'}$$ as $k[G_F]$-modules.
  - Explanation: Evaluating the free group-ring module at a character gives a stable rank-two lattice. Characters with the same residual evaluation give isomorphic residual representations, with no condition that the prime avoids the degree.
- **claim-congruences** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sys-congruences)): For every $t\in U(K)$ there are isomorphisms $$\label{sys:congruence-chain} \overline V_{\eta,p}\simeq\overline V_{\chi,p}, \qquad \overline V_{\chi,3}\simeq\overline V_{\psi,3}, \qquad \overline V_{\psi,p}\simeq\overline V_{1,p}.$$ These hold with the elliptic-curve realizations of Proposition <a href="#sys:elliptic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sys:elliptic">13</a> at the two ends.
  - Explanation: Three residual isomorphisms link the order-three system, the primitive system, the order-p system and the trivial system, so automorphy can pass along the chain.
- **claim-lifting-theorem** (theorem, [source](#stmt-aut-lift)): Let $L$ be a CM number field and $l$ an odd prime other than $5$. Let $r:G_L\to\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_2(\overline\mathbb Q_l)$ be continuous, unramified outside finitely many places, with determinant $\epsilon_l^{-1}$. Suppose that $r$ is potentially semistable with labeled Hodge–Tate weights $0,1$ at every $v\mid l$, that $\overline r$ is decomposed generic, and that $\overline r\|_{G_{L(\zeta_l)}}$ is absolutely irreducible.* *Suppose there are a cuspidal weight-zero regular algebraic representation $\pi$ of $\mathop{\mathrm{PGL}}_2(\mathbb A_L)$ and an isomorphism $\iota:\overline\mathbb Q_l\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb C$ for which $\overline{r_\iota(\pi)}\simeq\overline r$. At each $v\mid l$, assume one of the following:* 1) *Both local Galois representations are potentially crystalline; one is potentially ordinary of weight zero if and only if the other is; and $\mathop{\mathrm{rec}}_{L_v}(\pi_v)$ has zero monodromy.* 2) *Neither local Galois representation is potentially crystalline, and $\pi$ is $\iota$-ordinary of weight zero at $v$.* *Then $r\simeq r_\iota(\Pi)$ for a cuspidal weight-zero regular algebraic representation $\Pi$ of $\mathop{\mathrm{PGL}}_2(\mathbb A_L)$.
  - Explanation: A specialised form of the Caraiani-Newton lifting theorem: a Galois representation with the stated local behaviour and residual hypotheses, congruent to an automorphic one, is automorphic.
- **claim-elliptic-seed** (theorem, [source](#stmt-aut-seed)): Let $L$ be a CM number field not containing $\zeta_5$, and let $A/L$ be a non-CM elliptic curve. If its mod-$5$ representation is decomposed generic and is absolutely irreducible on $G_{L(\zeta_5)}$, then $H^1(A)$ is realized by a cuspidal weight-zero regular algebraic representation of $\mathop{\mathrm{PGL}}_2(\mathbb A_L)$.
  - Explanation: A non-CM elliptic curve over a CM field without a primitive fifth root of unity is modular when its mod-5 representation is decomposed generic and absolutely irreducible over the cyclotomic extension.
- **claim-elliptic-compat** (theorem, [source](#stmt-aut-elliptic-compat)): Let $D/L$ be a non-CM elliptic curve over a CM number field. Suppose that a regular algebraic cuspidal representation $\pi$ realizes $H^1(D)$ at one coefficient prime. Then $\pi$ has weight zero and trivial central character, realizes $H^1(D)$ at every coefficient prime and every coefficient isomorphism, and satisfies $$\iota\mathop{\mathrm{WD}}\bigl(H^1(D,\overline\mathbb Q_l)\|_{G_{L_v}}\bigr)^{\mathrm{F\text{-}ss}} \simeq\mathop{\mathrm{rec}}^T_{L_v}(\pi_v)\qquad(v\nmid l).$$ If $D$ has potentially multiplicative reduction at $v\mid l$, then $\pi$ is $\iota$-ordinary of weight zero there.
  - Explanation: Once a non-CM elliptic curve is realised at one coefficient prime, the automorphic representation realises it at all primes, matches local parameters and is ordinary at places of potentially multiplicative reduction.
- **claim-torsion-constancy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-torsion)): Let $L$ be a local field, let $X$ be a smooth $L$-variety, and let $\mathcal A\to X$ be an abelian scheme. For $x_0\in X(L)$ and an integer $N\geq 1$, there is an analytic neighborhood $\mathcal N$ of $x_0$ in $X(L)$ such that $$\mathcal A_x[N](\overline L)\simeq \mathcal A_{x_0}[N](\overline L) \qquad (x\in\mathcal N)$$ as $G_L$-modules.
  - Explanation: In characteristic zero, finite torsion Galois modules of an abelian scheme are locally constant on analytic neighbourhoods of a rational point of the base over a local field.
- **claim-frobenius-finite** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-frobenius)): Fix a local field $L$ with residue field of cardinality $Q$, an integer $g\geq 1$, and a prime $d$ different from the residue characteristic. For a semistable abelian variety $A/L$ of dimension $g$, let $$P_A(T)=\det\bigl(T-\operatorname{Frob}_L\mid V_d(A)\bigr),$$ where $\operatorname{Frob}_L$ is any lift of arithmetic Frobenius. Then $P_A(T)$ belongs to $\mathbb Z[T]$, is independent of the lift and of $d$, and ranges over a finite set depending only on $g$ and $Q$. Moreover, $P_A$ determines the toric rank of $A$; if that rank is zero, it also determines the Newton polygon of its good reduction.
  - Explanation: Frobenius polynomials on the Tate module of semistable abelian varieties of fixed dimension over a fixed local field form a finite set. They determine the toric rank and, in good reduction, the Newton polygon.
- **claim-semistable-constancy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-constancy)): Let $L_0$ be a local field, let $X$ be a smooth $L_0$-variety, and let $\mathcal A\to X$ be an abelian scheme of relative dimension $g$. For $x_0\in X(L_0)$, there exist a finite extension $L/L_0$ and an analytic neighborhood $\mathcal N\subset X(L_0)$ of $x_0$ with the following properties. For every $x\in\mathcal N$, the abelian variety $\mathcal A_x$ is semistable over $L$ and has the same toric rank as $\mathcal A_{x_0}$ over $L$. If $\mathcal A_{x_0}$ has good reduction over $L$, then $\mathcal A_x$ has the same Newton polygon over $L$.* *In particular, if $\mathcal A_{x_0}\simeq E_0^g$ for an elliptic curve $E_0/L_0$, every sufficiently nearby fiber has, after the same finite extension, the same one of the following three properties as $E_0^g$:* 1. *good ordinary reduction;* 2. *good reduction with all Newton slopes equal to $1/2$;* 3. *semistable reduction of toric rank $g$.
  - Explanation: Near a chosen fibre, nearby fibres of an abelian scheme are semistable over a fixed extension with the same toric rank and, in good reduction, the same Newton polygon.
- **claim-toric-monodromy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-toric-monodromy)): Let $L/\mathbb Q_\ell$ be finite and let $A/L$ be an abelian variety of dimension $g$. Suppose that $A$ acquires semistable reduction of toric rank $g$ after a finite extension. For every prime $d$, the monodromy operator on the Weil–Deligne representation of $H^1_{\mathrm{et}}(A_{\overline L},\mathbb Q_d)$ has rank $g$. For $d=\ell$, the Weil–Deligne representation is the one attached to the potentially semistable representation by $\ell$-adic Hodge theory.
  - Explanation: An abelian variety acquiring semistable reduction of full toric rank has monodromy of full rank on first cohomology at every coefficient prime, including the residue characteristic.
- **claim-local-opens** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-opens)): For every place $v_0\mid 3p$ of $K$, there is a nonempty open subset $\Omega_{v_0}\subset U(K_{v_0})$ with the following property. Suppose that $t\in U(K)$ belongs to $\Omega_{v_0}$ for all $v_0\mid3p$, and set $J_t=\mathop{\mathrm{Jac}}(C_{n,t})$. At each place $v\mid3p$ of $F$, $J_t$ has the same one of the following potential reduction types as $E^n$:* 1. *good ordinary reduction;* 2. *good reduction with all Newton slopes $1/2$;* 3. *semistable reduction of toric rank $n$.* *For each $v$, the extensions giving these properties may be chosen in advance, independently of $t$ in the indicated open subsets.
  - Explanation: For each place above the chosen primes there is a nonempty open set of parameters on which the Jacobian of the cover has the same potential reduction type as the product of elliptic curves.
- **claim-local-characters** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-characters)): Choose $t$ satisfying the open conditions of Proposition <a href="#loc:opens" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="loc:opens">24</a>. Let $v\mid3p$ be a place of $F$, of residue characteristic $\ell$. Then, simultaneously for every rotation character $\theta$, the following conclusions hold.* 1. *If $E$ has potentially good ordinary reduction at $v$, then $V_{\theta,\ell}\|_{G_{F_v}}$ is potentially crystalline and potentially ordinary of weight $0$.* 2. *If $E$ has potentially good supersingular reduction at $v$, then $V_{\theta,\ell}\|_{G_{F_v}}$ is potentially crystalline and is not potentially ordinary of weight $0$.* 3. *If $E$ has potentially multiplicative reduction at $v$, then the monodromy of $V_{\theta,d}\|_{G_{F_v}}$ has rank one for every coefficient prime $d$, including $d=\ell$. In particular, $V_{\theta,\ell}\|_{G_{F_v}}$ is not potentially crystalline.
  - Explanation: For parameters in the open sets, each character piece is potentially crystalline and potentially ordinary, or crystalline and not ordinary, or has monodromy of rank one and is not potentially crystalline, according to the reduction type of E.
- **claim-geometric-monodromy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-res-geometric)): For each pair in <a href="#res:pairs" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="res:pairs">[res:pairs]</a>, the geometric monodromy of $H^1(C_m,\overline\mathbb F_\ell)$ on every nontrivial rotation-character space is absolutely irreducible. Its image contains opposite nonidentity root unipotents on that space. Moreover, one element of the geometric monodromy acts regular semisimply on all these nontrivial character spaces simultaneously. These statements also hold for the conjugate family over $K$.
  - Explanation: On each nontrivial character space the geometric monodromy is absolutely irreducible, contains opposite nonidentity unipotents and contains one element regular semisimple on all such spaces at once.
- **claim-trivial-character** (theorem, [source](#stmt-res-trivial-character)): For every $t\in U(K)$, the representation $\overline V_{1,p}$ is absolutely irreducible on $G_{F(\zeta_p)}$ and is decomposed generic over $F$.
  - Explanation: The residual representation for the trivial character is absolutely irreducible and decomposed generic at every parameter, because the image on the p-torsion is large and a counting argument handles the conjugate curve.
- **claim-finite-specialization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-res-finite-specialization)): Let $W$ be a nonempty open subset of $\mathbb A^d_\mathbb Q$, let $D/\mathbb Q$ be a finite Galois extension, and let $H$ be a finite quotient of $\pi_1(W)$ through which the constant-field map factors as $$\pi_1(W)\twoheadrightarrow H\twoheadrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Gal}}(D/\mathbb Q).$$ Prescribe nonempty open subsets of $W(\mathbb Q_r)$ for finitely many rational primes $r$. There is a point $x\in W(\mathbb Q)$ in all the prescribed opens for which the specialization map $s_x:G_\mathbb Q\to H$ is surjective. For this point, $$\label{res:constant-kernel} s_x(G_D)=\ker\bigl(H\longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Gal}}(D/\mathbb Q)\bigr).$$
  - Explanation: Hilbert irreducibility with weak approximation gives a rational point in prescribed local open sets whose specialisation map onto a finite quotient is surjective, with the stated image on the constant-field kernel.
- **claim-specialization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-res-specialization)): Let $S_K$ be a finite set of finite places of $K$ containing all places above $3p$. For each $v_0\in S_K$, prescribe a nonempty open subset $\Omega_{v_0}\subset U(K_{v_0})$. There exists a single $t\in U(K)$ lying in every $\Omega_{v_0}$ such that the following assertions hold over $F=K(\mu_{3p})$.* 1. *For each $(m,\ell)$ in <a href="#res:pairs" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="res:pairs">[res:pairs]</a> and each character $\theta$ of order $m$, the representation $\overline V_{\theta,\ell}$ is absolutely irreducible on $G_{F(\zeta_\ell)}$ and is decomposed generic.* 2. *The representation $\overline V_{1,p}$ has these same two properties at $p$.* 3. *The elliptic curve $A_t=\mathop{\mathrm{Jac}}(C_3(t)/\langle w\rangle)$ is non-CM.* *In particular, when the opens above $3p$ are those of Proposition <a href="#loc:opens" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="loc:opens">24</a>, this same parameter also satisfies all the local conclusions of Corollary <a href="#loc:characters" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="loc:characters">25</a>.
  - Explanation: A single parameter exists in all prescribed local open sets for which the residual representations are absolutely irreducible and decomposed generic and the auxiliary elliptic curve has no complex multiplication.
- **claim-transport** (theorem, [source](#stmt-aut-transport)): Suppose a weight-zero regular algebraic cuspidal representation $\pi$ of $\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_2(\mathbb A_F)$ realizes one member of the compatible system $V_\theta$. Then it realizes every member, using the compatible coefficient embeddings fixed in Section <a href="#sec:systems" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:systems">5</a>.
  - Explanation: If an automorphic representation realises one member of the compatible system it realises every member, by equality of Frobenius polynomials, semisimplicity and Chebotarev density.
- **claim-automorphic-local** (theorem, [source](#stmt-aut-automorphic-local)): Suppose that a weight-zero regular algebraic cuspidal representation $\pi$ of $\mathop{\mathrm{PGL}}_2(\mathbb A_F)$ realizes $V_\theta$. Let $l\in\{3,p\}$ and $v\mid l$, and write $J_t=\mathop{\mathrm{Jac}}(C_n(t))$.* 1) *If $J_t$ has potentially good reduction at $v$, then $\mathop{\mathrm{rec}}_{F_v}(\pi_v)$ has zero monodromy.* 2) *If $J_t$ acquires semistable reduction of toric rank $n$ at $v$, then $\pi_v$ is a quadratic or trivial twist of the Steinberg representation, and $\pi$ is $\iota_l$-ordinary of weight zero at $v$.
  - Explanation: Good potential reduction forces zero monodromy in the local automorphic parameter. Totally toric reduction forces a twist of the Steinberg representation that is ordinary of weight zero.
- **claim-modular-over-F** (theorem, [source](#stmt-aut-over-f)): The elliptic curve $B/F$ is modular. More precisely, $H^1(B)$ is realized by a cuspidal weight-zero regular algebraic representation of $\mathop{\mathrm{PGL}}_2(\mathbb A_F)$.
  - Explanation: Starting from the modular elliptic curve at one end, three lifting steps along the congruence chain show that the twist of E is modular over the cyclotomic field F.
- **claim-descent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-aut-descent)): Let $L'/L$ be a cyclic extension of prime degree between CM fields. Let $r:G_L\to\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_2(\overline\mathbb Q_l)$ be a continuous representation whose restriction to $G_{L'}$ is absolutely irreducible. Suppose that this restriction is realized by a cuspidal weight-zero regular algebraic representation $\Pi$ of $\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_2(\mathbb A_{L'})$. Then $r$ is realized by a cuspidal weight-zero regular algebraic representation of $\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_2(\mathbb A_L)$.
  - Explanation: Automorphy descends along a cyclic prime-degree extension of CM fields for an absolutely irreducible restriction, using cyclic base change and a character twist.

        "anchor": "stmt-mon-transvections",
        "exact_quote": "In one integral basis $a_i,b_i$ as above, the geometric monodromy of the constant-$E$ family contains operators $T_a,T_b$ satisfying $$\\begin{aligned}\n T_a(a_j)&=a_j,&\n T_a(b_j)&=b_j+\\sigma_a(2a_j-a_{j-1}-a_{j+1}),\n \\label{mon:Ta}\\\\\n T_b(b_j)&=b_j,&\n T_b(a_j)&=a_j+\\sigma_b(2b_j-b_{j-1}-b_{j+1}),\n \\label{mon:Tb}\n\\end{aligned}$$ where $\\sigma_a,\\sigma_b\\in\\{1,-1\\}$ are independent of $j$. Equivalently, their two off-diagonal entries, in the basis of the free $\\mathbb Z[\\Gamma_m]$-module, are $\\sigma_a(2-u-u^{-1})$ and $\\sigma_b(2-u-u^{-1})$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-sys-properties",
        "exact_quote": "For every $t\\in U(K)$ and every rotation character $\\theta$, the representations $V_{\\theta,\\ell}$ have the following properties.*\n\n1.  *They are semisimple of dimension two, with determinant $\\epsilon_\\ell^{-1}$.*\n\n2.  *At every place of $F$ above $\\ell$, they are potentially semistable with labelled Hodge–Tate weights $\\{0,1\\}$.*\n\n3.  *There is a finite set of places $S$ of $F$, independent of $\\ell$, such that $V_{\\theta,\\ell}$ is unramified at every $v\\notin S$ of residue characteristic different from $\\ell$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-sys-elliptic",
        "exact_quote": "If $\\theta$ has order $m>1$ dividing $n$, the system $V_\\theta$ is realized by the corresponding character space in $H^1(C_m)$. For the trivial character, $$V_{1,\\ell}\\simeq H^1_{\\mathrm{\\acute et}}\n                  (B_{t,\\overline F},\\overline\\mathbb Q_\\ell).$$ Let $D_t=C_3(t)/\\langle w\\rangle$ and $A_t=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Jac}}(D_t)$. Then $D_t$ has genus one, $A_t$ is an elliptic curve over $F$, and for either nontrivial order-three character $\\eta$ there are $G_F$-equivariant isomorphisms $$V_{\\eta,\\ell}\\simeq\n H^1_{\\mathrm{\\acute et}}(A_{t,\\overline F},\\overline\\mathbb Q_\\ell)\n \\qquad\\text{for every }\\ell.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-030.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-sys-integral-evaluation",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $t\\in U(K)$ and a prime $\\ell$. Let $L/\\mathbb Q_\\ell$ be a finite extension containing $j_\\ell(M)$, with ring of integers $\\mathcal O$, maximal ideal $\\mathfrak m$, and residue field $k$. For a rotation character $\\theta$, the module $$\\mathcal T_\\theta=\n H^1_{\\mathrm{\\acute et}}(C_{n,\\overline F},\\mathbb Z_\\ell)\n \\otimes_{\\mathbb Z_\\ell[\\mu_n],\\,j_\\ell\\theta}\\mathcal O$$ is a free rank-two $G_F$-stable lattice realizing $V_{\\theta,\\ell}$ after extension to $\\overline\\mathbb Q_\\ell$. If $j_\\ell\\theta$ and $j_\\ell\\theta'$ have the same reduction as characters $\\mu_n\\to k^\\times$, then $$\\mathcal T_\\theta/\\mathfrak m\\mathcal T_\\theta\n \\simeq\n \\mathcal T_{\\theta'}/\\mathfrak m\\mathcal T_{\\theta'}$$ as $k[G_F]$-modules.",
        "source_id": "PM-030.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-sys-integral-evaluation",

For a rotation character $\\theta$, the module $$\\mathcal T_\\theta=\n H^1_{\\mathrm{\\acute et}}(C_{n,\\overline F},\\mathbb Z_\\ell)\n \\otimes_{\\mathbb Z_\\ell[\\mu_n],\\,j_\\ell\\theta}\\mathcal O$$ is a free rank-two $G_F$-stable lattice realizing $V_{\\theta,\\ell}$ after extension to $\\overline\\mathbb Q_\\ell$. If $j_\\ell\\theta$ and $j_\\ell\\theta'$ have the same reduction as characters $\\mu_n\\to k^\\times$, then $$\\mathcal T_\\theta/\\mathfrak m\\mathcal T_\\theta\n \\simeq\n \\mathcal T_{\\theta'}/\\mathfrak m\\mathcal T_{\\theta'}$$ as $k[G_F]$-modules.",
          "source_id": "PM-030.1"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-sys-congruences",
        "exact_quote": "For every $t\\in U(K)$ there are isomorphisms $$\\label{sys:congruence-chain}\n \\overline V_{\\eta,p}\\simeq\\overline V_{\\chi,p},\n \\qquad\n \\overline V_{\\chi,3}\\simeq\\overline V_{\\psi,3},\n \\qquad\n \\overline V_{\\psi,p}\\simeq\\overline V_{1,p}.$$ These hold with the elliptic-curve realizations of Proposition <a href=\"#sys:elliptic\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sys:elliptic\">13</a> at the two ends.",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-sys-congruences",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-aut-lift",
        "exact_quote": "Let $L$ be a CM number field and $l$ an odd prime other than $5$. Let $r:G_L\\to\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}_2(\\overline\\mathbb Q_l)$ be continuous, unramified outside finitely many places, with determinant $\\epsilon_l^{-1}$. Suppose that $r$ is potentially semistable with labeled Hodge–Tate weights $0,1$ at every $v\\mid l$, that $\\overline r$ is decomposed generic, and that $\\overline r|_{G_{L(\\zeta_l)}}$ is absolutely irreducible.*\n\n*Suppose there are a cuspidal weight-zero regular algebraic representation $\\pi$ of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{PGL}}_2(\\mathbb A_L)$ and an isomorphism $\\iota:\\overline\\mathbb Q_l\\xrightarrow{\\sim}\\mathbb C$ for which $\\overline{r_\\iota(\\pi)}\\simeq\\overline r$. At each $v\\mid l$, assume one of the following:*\n\n1)  *Both local Galois representations are potentially crystalline; one is potentially ordinary of weight zero if and only if the other is; and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{rec}}_{L_v}(\\pi_v)$ has zero monodromy.*\n\n2)  *Neither local Galois representation is potentially crystalline, and $\\pi$ is $\\iota$-ordinary of weight zero at $v$.*\n\n*Then $r\\simeq r_\\iota(\\Pi)$ for a cuspidal weight-zero regular algebraic representation $\\Pi$ of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{PGL}}_2(\\mathbb A_L)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-030.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-aut-lift",

At each $v\\mid l$, assume one of the following:*\n\n1)  *Both local Galois representations are potentially crystalline; one is potentially ordinary of weight zero if and only if the other is; and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{rec}}_{L_v}(\\pi_v)$ has zero monodromy.*\n\n2)  *Neither local Galois representation is potentially crystalline, and $\\pi$ is $\\iota$-ordinary of weight zero at $v$.*\n\n*Then $r\\simeq r_\\iota(\\Pi)$ for a cuspidal weight-zero regular algebraic representation $\\Pi$ of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{PGL}}_2(\\mathbb A_L)$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-aut-seed",
        "exact_quote": "Let $L$ be a CM number field not containing $\\zeta_5$, and let $A/L$ be a non-CM elliptic curve. If its mod-$5$ representation is decomposed generic and is absolutely irreducible on $G_{L(\\zeta_5)}$, then $H^1(A)$ is realized by a cuspidal weight-zero regular algebraic representation of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{PGL}}_2(\\mathbb A_L)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-030.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-030.1"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-aut-elliptic-compat",
        "exact_quote": "Let $D/L$ be a non-CM elliptic curve over a CM number field. Suppose that a regular algebraic cuspidal representation $\\pi$ realizes $H^1(D)$ at one coefficient prime.           "source_id": "PM-030.1"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-loc-torsion",
        "exact_quote": "Let $L$ be a local field, let $X$ be a smooth $L$-variety, and let $\\mathcal A\\to X$ be an abelian scheme.       "exact_statement": "Let $L$ be a local field, let $X$ be a smooth $L$-variety, and let $\\mathcal A\\to X$ be an abelian scheme.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-loc-frobenius",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a local field $L$ with residue field of cardinality $Q$, an integer $g\\geq 1$, and a prime $d$ different from the residue characteristic. For a semistable abelian variety $A/L$ of dimension $g$, let $$P_A(T)=\\det\\bigl(T-\\operatorname{Frob}_L\\mid V_d(A)\\bigr),$$ where $\\operatorname{Frob}_L$ is any lift of arithmetic Frobenius. Then $P_A(T)$ belongs to $\\mathbb Z[T]$, is independent of the lift and of $d$, and ranges over a finite set depending only on $g$ and $Q$. Moreover, $P_A$ determines the toric rank of $A$; if that rank is zero, it also determines the Newton polygon of its good reduction.",
        "source_id": "PM-030.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-loc-frobenius",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a local field $L$ with residue field of cardinality $Q$, an integer $g\\geq 1$, and a prime $d$ different from the residue characteristic. Then $P_A(T)$ belongs to $\\mathbb Z[T]$, is independent of the lift and of $d$, and ranges over a finite set depending only on $g$ and $Q$. Moreover, $P_A$ determines the toric rank of $A$; if that rank is zero, it also determines the Newton polygon of its good reduction.",
          "source_id": "PM-030.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix a local field $L$ with residue field of cardinality $Q$, an integer $g\\geq 1$, and a prime $d$ different from the residue characteristic. Then $P_A(T)$ belongs to $\\mathbb Z[T]$, is independent of the lift and of $d$, and ranges over a finite set depending only on $g$ and $Q$. Moreover, $P_A$ determines the toric rank of $A$; if that rank is zero, it also determines the Newton polygon of its good reduction.",
      "explanation": "Frobenius polynomials on the Tate module of semistable abelian varieties of fixed dimension over a fixed local field form a finite set. They determine the toric rank and, in good reduction, the Newton polygon.",

      "hypotheses": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-loc-constancy",
        "exact_quote": "Let $L_0$ be a local field, let $X$ be a smooth $L_0$-variety, and let $\\mathcal A\\to X$ be an abelian scheme of relative dimension $g$. For $x_0\\in X(L_0)$, there exist a finite extension $L/L_0$ and an analytic neighborhood $\\mathcal N\\subset X(L_0)$ of $x_0$ with the following properties. For every $x\\in\\mathcal N$, the abelian variety $\\mathcal A_x$ is semistable over $L$ and has the same toric rank as $\\mathcal A_{x_0}$ over $L$. If $\\mathcal A_{x_0}$ has good reduction over $L$, then $\\mathcal A_x$ has the same Newton polygon over $L$.*\n\n*In particular, if $\\mathcal A_{x_0}\\simeq E_0^g$ for an elliptic curve $E_0/L_0$, every sufficiently nearby fiber has, after the same finite extension, the same one of the following three properties as $E_0^g$:*\n\n1.  *good ordinary reduction;*\n\n2.  *good reduction with all Newton slopes equal to $1/2$;*\n\n3.  *semistable reduction of toric rank $g$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-loc-toric-monodromy",
        "exact_quote": "Let $L/\\mathbb Q_\\ell$ be finite and let $A/L$ be an abelian variety of dimension $g$. Suppose that $A$ acquires semistable reduction of toric rank $g$ after a finite extension. For every prime $d$, the monodromy operator on the Weil–Deligne representation of $H^1_{\\mathrm{et}}(A_{\\overline L},\\mathbb Q_d)$ has rank $g$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-loc-opens",
        "exact_quote": "For every place $v_0\\mid 3p$ of $K$, there is a nonempty open subset $\\Omega_{v_0}\\subset U(K_{v_0})$ with the following property. At each place $v\\mid3p$ of $F$, $J_t$ has the same one of the following potential reduction types as $E^n$:*\n\n1.  *good ordinary reduction;*\n\n2.  *good reduction with all Newton slopes $1/2$;*\n\n3.  *semistable reduction of toric rank $n$.*\n\n*For each $v$, the extensions giving these properties may be chosen in advance, independently of $t$ in the indicated open subsets.",

At each place $v\\mid3p$ of $F$, $J_t$ has the same one of the following potential reduction types as $E^n$:*\n\n1.  *good ordinary reduction;*\n\n2.  *good reduction with all Newton slopes $1/2$;*\n\n3.  *semistable reduction of toric rank $n$.*\n\n*For each $v$, the extensions giving these properties may be chosen in advance, independently of $t$ in the indicated open subsets.",
          "source_id": "PM-030.1"

      "exact_statement": "For every place $v_0\\mid 3p$ of $K$, there is a nonempty open subset $\\Omega_{v_0}\\subset U(K_{v_0})$ with the following property.         "anchor": "stmt-loc-characters",
        "exact_quote": "Choose $t$ satisfying the open conditions of Proposition <a href=\"#loc:opens\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"loc:opens\">24</a>. Let $v\\mid3p$ be a place of $F$, of residue characteristic $\\ell$. Then, simultaneously for every rotation character $\\theta$, the following conclusions hold.*\n\n1.  *If $E$ has potentially good ordinary reduction at $v$, then $V_{\\theta,\\ell}|_{G_{F_v}}$ is potentially crystalline and potentially ordinary of weight $0$.*\n\n2.  *If $E$ has potentially good supersingular reduction at $v$, then $V_{\\theta,\\ell}|_{G_{F_v}}$ is potentially crystalline and is not potentially ordinary of weight $0$.*\n\n3.  *If $E$ has potentially multiplicative reduction at $v$, then the monodromy of $V_{\\theta,d}|_{G_{F_v}}$ has rank one for every coefficient prime $d$, including $d=\\ell$.         "anchor": "stmt-res-finite-specialization",
        "exact_quote": "Let $W$ be a nonempty open subset of $\\mathbb A^d_\\mathbb Q$, let $D/\\mathbb Q$ be a finite Galois extension, and let $H$ be a finite quotient of $\\pi_1(W)$ through which the constant-field map factors as $$\\pi_1(W)\\twoheadrightarrow H\\twoheadrightarrow\\mathop{\\mathrm{Gal}}(D/\\mathbb Q).$$ Prescribe nonempty open subsets of $W(\\mathbb Q_r)$ for finitely many rational primes $r$.         "exact_quote": "Let $L'/L$ be a cyclic extension of prime degree between CM fields.       },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-aut-descent",
          "exact_quote": "Let $L'/L$ be a cyclic extension of prime degree between CM fields. Then $r$ is realized by a cuspidal weight-zero regular algebraic representation of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}_2(\\mathbb A_L)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-030.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $L'/L$ be a cyclic extension of prime degree between CM fields. Let $r:G_L\\to\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}_2(\\overline\\mathbb Q_l)$ be a continuous representation whose restriction to $G_{L'}$ is absolutely irreducible. Suppose that this restriction is realized by a cuspidal weight-zero regular algebraic representation $\\Pi$ of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}_2(\\mathbb A_{L'})$. Then $r$ is realized by a cuspidal weight-zero regular algebraic representation of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}_2(\\mathbb A_L)$.",
      "explanation": "Automorphy descends along a cyclic prime-degree extension of CM fields for an absolutely irreducible restriction, using cyclic base change and a character twist.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-aut-descent",
          "exact_quote": "Let $L'/L$ be a cyclic extension of prime degree between CM fields.",
          "source_id": "PM-030.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-aut-descent",
          "exact_quote": "Let $r:G_L\\to\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}_2(\\overline\\mathbb Q_l)$ be a continuous representation whose restriction to $G_{L'}$ is absolutely irreducible.",
          "source_id": "PM-030.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-aut-descent",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **decomposed generic** ([source](#stmt-def-decomposed-generic)): The representation is *decomposed generic* if there is a rational prime $q\ne l$ splitting completely in $L$ such that, for every $v\mid q$, it is unramified at $v$ and its Frobenius eigenvalues $\alpha_v,\beta_v$ satisfy $$\alpha_v/\beta_v\ne q,\qquad \beta_v/\alpha_v\ne q \quad\text{in }\overline\mathbb F_l.$$
- **group ring of the deck group** ([source](#sec:monodromy)): Throughout this section $\mathbb Z[\Gamma_m]$ denotes the *group ring*, not the cyclotomic integer ring.
- **weight zero** ([source](#eq:L-shift)): The algebraic weight corresponding to trivial coefficients is called *weight zero*.
- **potentially crystalline** ([source](#sec:conventions)): For a local field, a representation is *potentially crystalline* if it becomes crystalline after a finite extension.
- **potentially ordinary of weight zero** ([source](#sec:conventions)): A two-dimensional representation is *potentially ordinary of weight zero* if, after a finite extension, it admits an invariant line with unramified character and its quotient is $\epsilon_l^{-1}$ times an unramified character.
- **rotation-character realization V_theta** ([source](#sys:definition)): We write $V_\theta$ for the collection of these realizations.
- **compact type fibre** ([source](#pm-h-8)): Thus the fiber is of compact type: its identity-component Picard variety is the product of the Jacobians of its components, namely $E^m$.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-prime-p-bound | auxiliary prime p | > | $100$ | prime chosen by the open image theorem |
| q-cover-degree | covering degree n | = | $3p$ | p the auxiliary prime |
| q-genus-cover | genus of the fibre C_m | = | $m$ | divisors m>1 of n |
| q-riemann-hurwitz | twice the genus minus two of C_{m,t} | = | $2(m-1)$ | fibres over the parameter open set |
| q-semistable-nodes | number of geometric nodes of the special fibre | = | $m$ | either boundary parameter |
| q-homology-rank | rank of H_1 of a smooth fibre | = | $2m$ | divisors m of odd n |
| q-slit-parameter-bound | horizontal path parameter s | < | $\frac12$ | 0<s |
| q-determinant | determinant of V_{theta,ell} | = | $\epsilon_\ell^{-1}$ | every parameter t and every rotation character |
| q-hodge-tate-weights | labelled Hodge-Tate weights of V_{theta,ell} | = | $\{0,1\}$ | every parameter t and every rotation character |
| q-frobenius-coefficient-bound | absolute value of the coefficient of T^{2g-j} in the Frobenius polynomial | ≤ | $\binom{2g}{j}Q^j$ | all roots of absolute value at most Q |
| q-monodromy-rank | rank of monodromy for totally toric reduction | = | $g$ | every coefficient prime |
| q-toric-rank-jacobian | toric rank of the Jacobian at multiplicative places | = | $n$ | parameters in the local open sets |
| q-lambda-constant-order-three | coefficient c_lambda for characters of order three | = | $3$ | m=3 |
| q-trace-product | trace of the product of the two unipotent operators | = | $2+\sigma\tau k c_\lambda^2$ | integer k |
| q-order-bound-field | order of a root of unity of degree at most four over F_3 | ≤ | $80$ | prime p larger than 100 |
| q-trace-two-count | number of matrices of trace 2 in SL_2(F_p) | = | $p^2$ | odd prime p |
| q-sl2-order | order of SL_2(F_p) | = | $p(p^2-1)$ | prime p |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-kummer-family** [claim](#stmt-geo-family) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-family): Principal divisor via the group law, Kummer extension, Riemann-Hurwitz and local smoothness of the normalisation. -> smooth proper genus-m family with involution
- **step-semistable-models** [claim](#stmt-geo-semistable-models) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-semistable-models): Blow up the collision point, separate the sections, then normalise in the Kummer equation. -> regular semistable model with nodes of smoothing order one
- **step-compact-type** [claim](#stmt-geo-compact-type) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-compact-type): Explicit function on the exceptional line, tree dual graph, relative Picard theorem. -> abelian scheme extension with product fibre at the origin
- **step-collision** [claim](#stmt-geo-collision) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-collision): Local analytic computation of the node equation and an order computation for the unramified cover. -> special fibre with nodes of smoothing order one
- **step-slit** [claim](#stmt-mon-slit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mon-slit): Homotopy equivalence of the slit complement with the complement of a disk and isotopy in the parameter. -> cyclic gluing description of the cover
- **step-integral-module** [claim](#stmt-mon-integral-module) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mon-integral-module): Unimodular intersection pairing gives index one, then a perfect coefficient-of-identity pairing for the dual. -> free rank-two group-ring modules
- **step-vanishing-classes** [claim](#stmt-mon-vanishing-classes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mon-vanishing-classes): Intersection numbers of a lifted boundary circle with the basis loops. -> vanishing classes as differences of adjacent horizontal cycles
- **step-transvections** [claim](#stmt-mon-transvections) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mon-transvections): Picard-Lefschetz formula, then comparison of the two bases through a common trivialisation near the origin. -> two operators in one integral basis
- **step-system-properties** [claim](#stmt-sys-properties) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sys-properties): Perfect alternating pairing, Hodge counts, comparison theorems, trace formula and semisimplicity. -> two-dimensional semisimple compatible systems
- **step-elliptic-realizations** [claim](#stmt-sys-elliptic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sys-elliptic): Pullback along quotient covers and invariants under the involution. -> elliptic-curve realisations of the trivial and order-three systems
- **step-integral-evaluation** [claim](#stmt-sys-integral-evaluation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sys-integral-evaluation): Tensor the free group-ring module with a character evaluation and reduce. -> stable lattices with residual dependence only on the reduced character
- **step-congruences** [claim](#stmt-sys-congruences) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sys-congruences): Roots of unity of power order reduce to one, then apply integral evaluation. -> chain of residual isomorphisms
- **step-lifting-theorem** [claim](#stmt-aut-lift) / [proof](#stmt-aut-lift): Quoted specialised form of an external modularity lifting theorem. -> automorphy of the lifted representation
- **step-elliptic-seed** [claim](#stmt-aut-seed) / [proof](#stmt-aut-seed): Quoted external theorem on modularity of a starting elliptic curve. -> modularity of the auxiliary elliptic curve
- **step-elliptic-compat** [claim](#stmt-aut-elliptic-compat) / [proof](#stmt-aut-elliptic-compat): Quoted external compatibility result for modular elliptic curves. -> local-global compatibility for the elliptic curve
- **step-torsion-constancy** [claim](#stmt-loc-torsion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-loc-torsion): Finite etale torsor and the inverse function theorem, shrinking to be Galois stable. -> local constancy of finite torsion
- **step-frobenius-finite** [claim](#stmt-loc-frobenius) / [proof](#proof-stmt-loc-frobenius): Semistable filtration, bounds on roots and coefficients, finiteness of integral polynomials. -> finite set of Frobenius polynomials determining toric rank
- **step-semistable-constancy** [claim](#stmt-loc-constancy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-loc-constancy): Unipotent inertia from rational torsion, then congruent Frobenius polynomials from deeper torsion. -> constant toric rank and Newton polygon on a neighbourhood
- **step-toric-monodromy** [claim](#stmt-loc-toric-monodromy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-loc-toric-monodromy): Toric uniformisation, valuation pairing, and Kummer classes with Bloch-Kato for the residue characteristic. -> monodromy of full rank
- **step-local-opens** [claim](#stmt-loc-opens) / [proof](#proof-stmt-loc-opens): Apply local constancy at the origin and transfer the neighbourhood to the original parameter via an analytic lift. -> nonempty local open sets of parameters
- **step-local-characters** [claim](#stmt-loc-characters) / [proof](#proof-stmt-loc-characters): Crystalline comparison, ordinary filtration, slope argument and rank count for monodromy on the summands. -> local properties of every character summand
- **step-geometric-monodromy** [claim](#stmt-res-geometric) / [proof](#proof-stmt-res-geometric): Explicit matrices for the two transvections, invariant lines and trace computation. -> irreducibility and regular semisimple element
- **step-trivial-character** [claim](#stmt-res-trivial-character) / [proof](#proof-stmt-res-trivial-character): Large image on p-torsion, count of matrices of trace plus or minus two, Chebotarev density. -> irreducibility and decomposed genericity for the trivial character
- **step-finite-specialization** [claim](#stmt-res-finite-specialization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-res-finite-specialization): Hilbert irreducibility with weak approximation for a finite etale Galois cover. -> rational point with surjective specialisation
- **step-specialization** [claim](#stmt-res-specialization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-res-specialization): Restriction of scalars, finite monodromy quotient, Hilbert specialisation, Chebotarev, and a commutator argument against CM. -> single parameter satisfying residual and local conditions
- **step-transport** [claim](#stmt-aut-transport) / [proof](#proof-stmt-aut-transport): Equality of Frobenius polynomials, Chebotarev density and Brauer-Nesbitt. -> automorphy at every coefficient prime
- **step-automorphic-local** [claim](#stmt-aut-automorphic-local) / [proof](#proof-stmt-aut-automorphic-local): Compare Weil representations at a different coefficient prime, then use genericity and the Steinberg classification. -> local automorphic conditions at the next lift
- **step-modular-over-F** [claim](#stmt-aut-over-f) / [proof](#proof-stmt-aut-over-f): Seed modularity followed by three applications of the lifting theorem along the congruence chain. -> modularity of the twist over F
- **step-descent** [claim](#stmt-aut-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-aut-descent): Strong multiplicity one, cyclic base change, Schur lemma and a character twist. -> descent of automorphy along cyclic extensions
- **step-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#pm-h-31): Descend along the tower, untwist, apply compatibility for local parameters, and treat the CM case by induction or isobaric sum. -> automorphic representation with matching local parameters

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-main-modularity**: For every imaginary quadratic field K and every elliptic curve E over K, an isobaric automorphic representation of GL_2 over K with trivial central character and weight zero realizes the etale cohomology of E and matches all local parameters.
- **inv-congruence-chain**: For every parameter t in U(K), the residual representations of the order-three, order-3p, order-p and trivial character systems are linked by the stated chain of isomorphisms, with elliptic-curve realizations at both ends.
- **inv-trace-two-count**: The group of determinant-one 2x2 matrices over the field with p elements contains exactly p squared matrices of trace 2, which feeds the counting argument for regular semisimple elements.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-other-fields**: Apply the main theorem to elliptic curves over cubic fields, totally real fields or other number fields. -> The statement and its proof machinery are built for an imaginary quadratic base field; no conclusion follows for other fields.
- **anti-explicit-computation**: Treat the theorem as an explicit procedure that outputs the automorphic representation or its Hecke data from the curve. -> The proof selects a parameter by Hilbert irreducibility and Chebotarev-type existence arguments, which give existence and not a stated finite algorithm or cost bound.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main results are existence and compatibility statements obtained from automorphy lifting, Hilbert irreducibility and Chebotarev arguments; the source states no finite procedure, explicit algorithm or complexity bound whose output could be executed and checked, so no reference implementation or oracle is given.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The main theorem is an existence and compatibility statement for automorphic representations; it gives no finite procedure that a program could execute to build the representation. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the paper concerns simulation, dynamics or interactive systems; it deals with Galois representations and covers of elliptic curves. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result can only justify a tool's correctness claim about L-functions; it supplies no tool-level algorithm, and the cited computations belong to other works. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The proof uses Hilbert irreducibility, Chebotarev density and lifting theorems, all existential, so no algorithm with stated complexity arises. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper introduces no data organisation; the integral module over the group ring is a mathematical lattice, not a storage structure. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No computation, circuit or arithmetic kernel is described, so there is nothing to map onto hardware. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Elliptic curves appear only as objects over number fields; no protocol, hardness assumption or security claim is made. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No learning, data fitting or statistical estimation occurs anywhere in the argument. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No objective function or feasible-set optimisation is treated; the choice of parameter is by existence, not extremisation. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper has no notion of nodes, messages or consensus; the word local refers to completions of number fields. |
| other (arithmetic geometry and automorphic forms (pure mathematics)) | True | strong | inspirational | inferred | The paper's real home is pure mathematics: it proves modularity of every elliptic curve over an imaginary quadratic field by lifting automorphy along a chain of congruences between character pieces of a Jacobian family. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-imaginary-quadratic-base** (hypothesis): The main theorem is stated only for an imaginary quadratic number field K and an elliptic curve E over K. Nothing in the statement covers other base fields. Blocked use: ['Applying the theorem to elliptic curves over cubic, totally real or other number fields', 'Applying the theorem to higher-dimensional abelian varieties']
- **limit-lifting-hypotheses** (hypothesis): The lifting theorem needs a CM field, an odd prime other than 5, and a continuous representation with determinant the inverse cyclotomic character. These conditions must hold before it is invoked. Blocked use: ['Invoking the lifting step at the prime 5', 'Invoking it over a base field that is not CM']
- **limit-seed-no-fifth-roots** (hypothesis): The elliptic starting point requires a CM field not containing a primitive fifth root of unity and a non-CM curve. The mod-5 representation must also be decomposed generic and absolutely irreducible over the cyclotomic extension. Blocked use: ['Using the seed modularity result when the field contains the fifth roots of unity', 'Using it for a CM elliptic curve']
- **limit-decomposed-generic** (hypothesis): Decomposed genericity asks for one rational prime different from the residual characteristic that splits completely and gives distinct Frobenius eigenvalue ratios at every place above it. A single unchecked place breaks the property. Blocked use: ['Declaring a residual representation decomposed generic after checking only some places above q']
- **limit-characteristic-zero-family** (formal_scope): The abelian scheme extension over the parameter curve is established in characteristic zero only. No integral model of the Kummer cover is claimed where the residue characteristic divides the degree. Blocked use: ['Reading the compact-type extension as a statement about reduction of the cover at primes dividing m']
- **limit-nonconstructive-parameter** (nonconstructive): The parameter t is produced by Hilbert irreducibility with weak approximation. The result gives existence of a rational point and no explicit search bound. Blocked use: ['Treating the choice of t as a bounded search procedure', 'Using the theorem as an algorithm to compute the automorphic representation']
- **limit-local-opens-only-above-3p** (edge_case): The local reduction types are controlled only at places above 3p, through nonempty open sets of parameters. Other places are handled by a finite exceptional set and are not given the same control. Blocked use: ['Assuming the same potential reduction type of the Jacobian at places away from 3p']
- **limit-descent-cyclic-prime** (hypothesis): Descent applies one cyclic prime-degree step between CM fields at a time and needs absolute irreducibility of the restriction to the larger field. Blocked use: ['Descending along a non-cyclic or non-prime-degree extension in a single step', 'Descending a representation whose restriction is reducible']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the modularity conjecture for elliptic curves over imaginary quadratic fields: every such curve is modular, with matching local parameters at every place.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>

| anchor | kind | label | number | first sentence |
|---|---|---|---|---|

| [stmt-geo-family](#stmt-geo-family) | lemma | `geo:family` | 2 | For each divisor $m>1$ of $n$, there is a smooth proper family of geometrically connected genus-$m$ curves $\mathcal C_m\to U$. |
| [stmt-geo-semistable-models](#stmt-geo-semistable-models) | lemma | `geo:semistable-models` | 3 | In the constant-$E$ family, blow up the surface at the collision point over either boundary parameter in [geo:boundary-points], and normalize the resulting surface in $Y^m=f_R$. |
| [stmt-geo-compact-type](#stmt-geo-compact-type) | proposition | `geo:compact-type` | 4 | Suppose that $k$ contains $\mu_m$. |
| [stmt-geo-characteristic-zero](#stmt-geo-characteristic-zero) | remark | `geo:characteristic-zero` | 5 | Proposition 4 concerns an abelian scheme over an open subset of the characteristic-zero parameter curve $E_k$. |
| [stmt-geo-collision](#stmt-geo-collision) | lemma | `geo:collision` | 6 | For $R_*=a/(2n)$, the special fiber in Lemma 3 consists of a connected unramified degree-$m$ cover $E'\to E$ and a rational component, joined at $m$ distinct nodes. |
| [stmt-mon-slit](#stmt-mon-slit) | lemma | `mon:slit` | 7 | For the parameters in [mon:horizontal-path], the cover $C_{m,R_s}\to E$ is trivial over $E\setminus I_s$. |
| [stmt-mon-integral-module](#stmt-mon-integral-module) | proposition | `mon:integral-module` | 8 | The cycles $a_i,b_i$ form an integral symplectic basis of $H_1(C_{m,R_s},\mathbb Z)$. |
| [stmt-auto-remark-1](#stmt-auto-remark-1) | remark | - | 9 | The compact-type degeneration gives another explanation of the integral statement. |
| [stmt-mon-vanishing-classes](#stmt-mon-vanishing-classes) | lemma | `mon:vanishing-classes` | 10 | After a cyclic indexing of the lifts, their homology classes are |
| [stmt-mon-transvections](#stmt-mon-transvections) | theorem | `mon:transvections` | 11 | In one integral basis $a_i,b_i$ as above, the geometric monodromy of the constant-$E$ family contains operators $T_a,T_b$ satisfying |
| [stmt-sys-properties](#stmt-sys-properties) | proposition | `sys:properties` | 12 | For every $t\in U(K)$ and every rotation character $\theta$, the representations $V_{\theta,\ell}$ have the following properties.* |
| [stmt-sys-elliptic](#stmt-sys-elliptic) | proposition | `sys:elliptic` | 13 | If $\theta$ has order $m>1$ dividing $n$, the system $V_\theta$ is realized by the corresponding character space in $H^1(C_m)$. |
| [stmt-sys-integral-evaluation](#stmt-sys-integral-evaluation) | lemma | `sys:integral-evaluation` | 14 | Fix $t\in U(K)$ and a prime $\ell$. |
| [stmt-sys-congruences](#stmt-sys-congruences) | corollary | `sys:congruences` | 15 | For every $t\in U(K)$ there are isomorphisms |
| [stmt-def-decomposed-generic](#stmt-def-decomposed-generic) | definition | `def:decomposed-generic` | 16 | Let $L$ be a number field and let $\overline r:G_L\to\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_2(\overline\mathbb F_l)$ be continuous. |
| [stmt-aut-lift](#stmt-aut-lift) | theorem | `aut:lift` | 17 | Let $L$ be a CM number field and $l$ an odd prime other than $5$. |
| [stmt-aut-seed](#stmt-aut-seed) | theorem | `aut:seed` | 18 | Let $L$ be a CM number field not containing $\zeta_5$, and let $A/L$ be a non-CM elliptic curve. |
| [stmt-aut-elliptic-compat](#stmt-aut-elliptic-compat) | theorem | `aut:elliptic-compat` | 19 | Let $D/L$ be a non-CM elliptic curve over a CM number field. |
| [stmt-loc-torsion](#stmt-loc-torsion) | lemma | `loc:torsion` | 20 | Let $L$ be a local field, let $X$ be a smooth $L$-variety, and let $\mathcal A\to X$ be an abelian scheme. |
| [stmt-loc-frobenius](#stmt-loc-frobenius) | lemma | `loc:frobenius` | 21 | Fix a local field $L$ with residue field of cardinality $Q$, an integer $g\geq 1$, and a prime $d$ different from the residue characteristic. |
| [stmt-loc-constancy](#stmt-loc-constancy) | proposition | `loc:constancy` | 22 | Let $L_0$ be a local field, let $X$ be a smooth $L_0$-variety, and let $\mathcal A\to X$ be an abelian scheme of relative dimension $g$. |

| [stmt-res-geometric](#stmt-res-geometric) | lemma | `res:geometric` | 26 | For each pair in [res:pairs], the geometric monodromy of $H^1(C_m,\overline\mathbb F_\ell)$ on every nontrivial rotation-character space is absolutely irreducible. |
| [stmt-res-trivial-character](#stmt-res-trivial-character) | lemma | `res:trivial-character` | 27 | For every $t\in U(K)$, the representation $\overline V_{1,p}$ is absolutely irreducible on $G_{F(\zeta_p)}$ and is decomposed generic over $F$.* |
| [stmt-res-finite-specialization](#stmt-res-finite-specialization) | lemma | `res:finite-specialization` | 28 | Let $W$ be a nonempty open subset of $\mathbb A^d_\mathbb Q$, let $D/\mathbb Q$ be a finite Galois extension, and let $H$ be a finite quotient of $\pi_1(W)$ through which the constant-field map factor |
| [stmt-res-specialization](#stmt-res-specialization) | proposition | `res:specialization` | 29 | Let $S_K$ be a finite set of finite places of $K$ containing all places above $3p$. |
